=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N buildings arranged in a line at equal intervals. The height of the i-th building from the front is H_i.
You want to decorate some of these buildings with illuminations so that both of the following conditions are satisfied:

- The chosen buildings all have the same height.
- The chosen buildings are arranged at equal intervals.

What is the maximum number of buildings you can choose? If you choose exactly one building, it is considered to satisfy the conditions.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
H_1 \ldots H_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 3000
- 1 \leq H_i \leq 3000
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

8
5 7 5 7 7 5 7 7

Sample Output 1

3

Choosing the 2nd, 5th, and 8th buildings from the front satisfies the conditions.

Sample Input 2

10
100 200 300 400 500 600 700 800 900 1000

Sample Output 2

1

Choosing just one building is considered to satisfy the conditions.

Sample Input 3

32
3 1 4 1 5 9 2 6 5 3 5 8 9 7 9 3 2 3 8 4 6 2 6 4 3 3 8 3 2 7 9 5

Sample Output 3

3

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    H = data[1:1+N]
    ans = 1
    for d in range(1, N):
        L = [0] * N
        # maybe first d entries = 1
        for i in range(N):
            if i >= d and H[i] == H[i-d]:
                L[i] = L[i-d] + 1
            else:
                L[i] = 1
            if L[i] > ans:
                ans = L[i]
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, final code. Need maybe include no comments? Fine. Code block:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    H = data[1:1+N]

    ans = 1
    for d in range(1, N):
        dp = [1] * N
        for i in range(d, N):
            if H[i] == H[i - d]:
                dp[i] = dp[i - d] + 1
                if dp[i] > ans:
                    ans = dp[i]

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

So if there is a longer AP ending at i-d with height H[i-d], dp[i-d] would be that length. If there is a longer AP ending at i-d with a different height, H[i] would have to equal that different height, but then H[i] != H[i-d], so condition false. Thus no issue.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    H = data[1:1 + N]

    ans = 1
    for d in range(1, N):
        dp = [1] * N
        for i in range(d, N):
            if H[i] == H[i - d]:
                dp[i] = dp[i - d] + 1
                if dp[i] > ans:
                    ans = dp[i]

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
